24e:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["Reject mixing two different operators from one precedence group without parentheses when the operators do not share a precedence. The groups mirror ESLint’s defaults — arithmetic (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"+ - * / % **"}],"), bitwise (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"& | ^ ~ << >> >>>"}],"), comparison (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"== != === !== > >= < <="}],"), logical (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"&& ||"}],"), and relational (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"in instanceof"}],") — and the rule fires only when both operators fall in the same group."]}]
24f:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["The famous case is ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"a && b || c"}],": readers expect left-to-right grouping but the parser sees ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"(a && b) || c"}]," because ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"&&"}]," binds tighter than ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"||"}],". Arithmetic mixes such as ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"a + b * c"}]," are reported for the same reason. Cross-group pairs (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"a | b && c"}],") and same-precedence pairs (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"a + b - c"}],") are left alone."]}]
